ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u42	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government draft law on the Development Fund for public service bus services. Today we present a bill that will allow Poles to recover important bus connections. It is an element - I cannot not say it - of fighting the rule of law and justice against the exclusion of communication. I say this with full awareness, because today, the exclusion of communication is a problem of hundreds, thousands, millions of Poles who, in our actions, look to the hope of changing their situation when it comes to mobility when it comes to moving on to the most important issues of life: trips and return to their places of residence. Our fight against communication exclusion in road transport is based on two pillars. The first is the Self-Government Road Fund. This fund already works for local communities. For 10 years we will help local governments with the amount of PLN 36 billion. I'm talking about the Self-Government Road Fund. The first funds have already gone to individual voivodships. All in order for the standard of life throughout Poland to improve systematically. Everyone in Poland has the right to access good and safe infrastructure regardless of which part of the country it comes from. The Fund for Self-Government Roads is thousands, tens of thousands, hundreds of thousands of kilometres of modern local roads throughout Poland, especially safe roads, predictable roads. Local authorities can also benefit from funding today, which could not afford to do so before. We have enabled local governments to carry out multiannual investments so that actions can be planned more in advance. The Self-Government Road Fund is therefore a strong pillar of the government's fight against communication exclusion. The second pillar is the development fund for bus transport, which we hope to establish after the adoption of the bill presented. For many years, Poland has been convinced that only those bus connections that are profitable and profitable are needed. It was believed that the invisible hand of the market is able to lead to the elimination of white spots on the map of Poland. The accidents of recent years, the experiences of recent years have shown that this is a wrong approach, it was a wrong approach. Other bus connections were cancelled because the operators were unable to generate profit from this activity, while local authorities alarmed that more and more towns were deprived of collective transport. The inhabitants of Polish towns, powiats and communes struggled with increasing communication exclusion. The exclusion of communication has a very practical, human and daily dimension. The exclusion of communication is a lack of access to work, difficulties in getting to school, to a doctor, to friends, to a family, a sense of powerlessness and leaving the country, which should take care of these matters. Disregarding the problem of communication exclusion, disregarding the needs of the smaller townspeople is a sad picture of previous governments. We're changing that today. Today, you finally take responsibility for the fight against communication exclusion. That is why we are creating a fund that will finance local bus connections, we are creating a development fund for bus transport, with the full conviction that we are gaining at every meeting with the people of our country in every region of the country. This belief that Poland should be built equal opportunities, Poland sustainable development, Poland where no one feels excluded because of the region of origin or the wealth of the wallet. Because Poland is one and that is what we keep saying. In every corner of our country, in every municipality, Poles live in each district and they have the right to feel that the Polish state remembers each one of them. The ambitions and life plans of every citizen of our country are unique. The Polish State cannot stand in the way and block their implementation. Politicians must therefore create a law that will make it easier for Poles from every region of the country to realize their plans and dreams. This is what we should always do as politicians in order to make life easier for the citizens of our country step by step. We want Poles in every region of the country to have good access to collective transport. In this way, we will contribute to the creation of new jobs, investment and sustainable development. We'll also serve the environment eventually. The fight against communication exclusion is also an effort to ensure that the car is not the only means of transport available in smaller and small towns. A coherent, predictable and user-friendly network of bus connections will enable more and more people to opt out of car transport. In order for such networks to arise, we are setting up a development fund for bus transport. Only this year will we help local authorities starting bus connections with the amount of PLN 300 million. In the following years it will amount to PLN 800 million. The grant will be granted in the form of a subsidy to the amount of the deficit of individual public utility lines. The sources of funding for this fund - and I ask you very much, ladies and gentlemen, the High House, and all of you, are: Fuel fee, that's a contribution to the fuel charge. No: increased fuel charge, no: another fee imposed on drivers. The fuel levy, which has been practically unchanged and unchanged for years, is charged. How will this work in this case? The revenue from the fuel levy will be increased by reducing revenue to the State budget for excise duties. By reducing revenue to the State budget for excise duties. Therefore, the funding of the bus development fund by means of the fuel levy will not reduce revenues to the National Road Fund and the Railway Fund. These sizes will not be affected. Neither the driver nor the National Road Fund nor the Railway Fund will be affected. The second component is the issue fee. The total amount of the issue fee shall not be amended. The amendment concerns the redirection of part of the revenue from this levy from the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and the Low-carbon Transport Fund to the Bus Development Fund. And I would like to ask, as regards what is very clear here, that this be the subject of a possible discussion, a debate, so that there is no need for further clarification as to the fact that the issue fee will be increased, that the subsequent rules which will introduce the Law and Justice will create any further burdens. Finally, it's a substitute fee. This is a substitute fee paid by an entity pursuing a national indicative objective, i.e. a trader engaged in an economic activity in the manufacture, import or acquisition of liquid fuels or liquid biofuels within the Community, where the entity has not fully achieved its national indicative objective. Another thing. State budget. It's kind of natural. The fund will also consist of revenue from penalties, fines, fees and general permits of the road transport inspector, as well as the provincial inspectors. At present, these revenues constitute revenue from the State budget. And I would like to escape further arguments that will appear in the discussion: there will be no plan to impose penalties, there will be no plan to impose penalties on truck drivers, all other drivers. We do not repeat the scenario, as we remember, Minister Vincent-Rostowski, who assumed in the draft budget that PLN 1600 million would enter the state budget due to the use of a large number of cameras. We've described revenue, revenue from these penalties to the fund based on the average of recent years. And this average of the last few years we've been eating. The breakdown of funds in a given financial year per voivodship will be carried out under the Regulation of the Council of Ministers on the allocation of funds of the Fund. By the end of 2021, which is extremely important, the distribution criteria will be: the area of the voivodship, the number of its inhabitants, the gross domestic product value and the meeting of the transport needs of the community. From 2022 onwards, the funds of the Fund will be allocated on the basis of operating work on public utility lines as defined in the transport plans of the various voivodships, excluding public transport. We can therefore talk about a transition period that will end in 2021. Until then, a surcharge of up to 1 PLN to 1 tonne-kilometre will apply. Repeat: up to 1 PLN to 1 car kilometer. We want this amount to be an incentive for local authorities to start transport where necessary is the most urgent. Later, this amount will not exceed 80 gr. Later, I mean. After 2021 What is the condition for the fund to be paid? The fund's contribution will be to finance a deficit amount on a single line of general interest of not less than 10% from the organiser's own resources. and the conclusion of a contract for the provision of public collective transport services. So I point out very clearly here: not as we originally proposed in the public debate prior to the submission of the project to the Council of Ministers, 30% from the local government, but 10% from the government. We have significantly reduced this liquidity threshold by the local government. The Voivodeship will control the manner and timeliness of the performance of the task for which the payment has been granted. The services of the voivodships will also determine whether the calculation of the aid has been carried out correctly in terms of compliance with the aid agreement. The mechanism of operation of the Fund is therefore simple: local authorities will start bus lines by concluding a contract for transport services with entrepreneurs, then report to the voivods what amount of aid is necessary to cover the deficit. We hope that, over time, running bus lines will become more profitable. This can only be achieved by repeating courses and a good route arrangement. The inhabitants of a given municipality or district will be more likely to board a bus or bus when they find that it will stay with them for longer, on the same route and during the same hours. Then money from the budget that has so far been spent on this line, where the deficit has been regulated, can be used to create another line. Experience shows that, where bus transport was launched on deficit lines, these deficit lines were very often turned into deficit lines, because passengers returned as they did today, to the railroad, to the bus, to the bus lines. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u55	I would now like to ask the floor of Małgorzata Zwierzan, Free and Solidarni.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u56	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position towards the Government's draft law on the Development Fund for public service bus services, print No 3411. This project regulates matters related to the creation and operation of a special fund, from which funds will be financed to restore local bus connections. This solution is intended to support local government units as organisers of public collective transport in fulfilling one of their own tasks. This applies to lines that have not been in operation for at least 3 months. The development fund for bus transport to be set up in the formula of the special purpose fund will be the responsibility of the minister responsible for transport. The proceeds to the fund will come from the state budget, the proceeds from the issue fee, the substitute fee, the fuel levy, the proceeds from penalties, fines, the fees charged by the Chief Inspector of Road Transport and the Provincial Road Transport Inspectors, as well as the permits issued by those authorities. In 2019 the level of funding of the fund's tasks will be PLN 300 million and in the following years PLN 800 million. The allocation of funds by regulation will be determined by the Council of Ministers. The aid will be granted in the form of a subsidy to the deficit amount of a single public service line. The project includes mechanisms to ensure uniform conditions for receiving aid. This solution is part of a package called the PiS 5 announced in March this year. The project we are looking at is another one, right after the 13th pension and the 500+ programme, which will include all children since July this year as an example of fulfilling their promises by the United Right Government, not just those electoral ones. The solution contained in the project in my opinion will have a significant impact on equalising social opportunities. Currently, in Poland there is a problem with ensuring that villagers and smaller towns can participate equally in public and social life. People living in towns far away from the centres of larger agglomerations often do not have free access to their infrastructure. The elimination of unprofitable bus connections carried out by private carriers and the lack of sufficient resources to maintain public lines make often the only possible form of transport to work, school or even doctor is their own car for these people. As we know, not all of the various reasons have the opportunity to use this form of transport. I am convinced that the funding provided for under these regulations for the development of coach transport will make normal everyday life easier for many Poles. About Free and Solidarni supports the presented project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u144	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! I present on behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications a report on the government draft law amending the Family and Care Code Act and the Code of Civil Procedure Act. These are 3295 and 3394 prints. The reasons for the amendment are indicated by the Council of Ministers in the justification for the project. This is the necessity of implementing the Constitutional Court ruling of 26 November 2013, following which Article. 71 of the family code, according to which the denial of the fatherhood was not admissible after the death of the child, lost effect, and of the second ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal, and more specifically the ruling of the Court of 11 February 2014, which indicates the need to introduce a regulation ensuring that the guardian is properly qualified to represent the child in criminal proceedings in the event of a conflict of interest between his parents and to impose information obligations on that guardian in relation to the parent who is caring for the child. Marshal of the Sejm at its 79th meeting, held on 4 April 2019, pursuant to Article. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed this draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for consideration. The committee meeting was held on 24 April. It was attended by Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice Łukasz Piebiak, together with colleagues, the curator of the court of Aleksandra Dźwigulska, the staff of the office and advisors of the Extraordinary Commission, as well as legislators from the Legislative Office. Mr Piebiak presented the draft to the Undersecretary of State at the committee meeting. There was a discussion about the project. All the provisions of the submission have been systematically discussed. Cooling and legislative amendments have been tabled and adopted. Nine Members voted in favour, no one opposed, no one abstained. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u145	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. As the first Mrs Barbara Bartus, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u146	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! I am pleased to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Family and Care Code Act and the Civil Procedure Code Act contained in print no. 3394. I would like to remind you that the Government's print project No 3295 concerns the need to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 26 November 2013, as a result of which Article. 71 of the Family and Care Code, according to which the denial of paternity was not admissible after the death of the child, has expired, and the implementation of the order of the Constitutional Tribunal of 11 February 2014, which indicated the need to introduce a regulation ensuring that the guardian is properly qualified to represent the child in criminal proceedings in the event of a conflict of interest between his parents and to impose information obligations on that curator in relation to the parent in charge of the child. As regards the modification of the loss of power by Article. 71 The family and caring code is in the draft attached to the report we depart from the existing principle of stabilisation of the child's marital status after his death, because the new regulation introduces exceptions to this rule relating to the situation where the child died after the initiation of the procedure concerning the establishment of motherhood or fatherhood. This has not been the case so far, but this regulation will enable the court to establish family ties according to the actual biological origin of the child by completing proceedings for the denial of fatherhood, the denial of motherhood and the determination of the ineffectiveness of the recognition of the child initiated in the life of the child and the determination of motherhood or fatherhood by judicial means after the death of the child. As regards the need for changes in the statutory definition of professional requirements for guardians established for the representation of a child in criminal proceedings, it has so far been pointed out that conflict curators have failed to comply with their obligations. He pointed to this, among other things. in his report, the spokesperson for the rights of the child. However, the role of the curator, even in criminal proceedings, is very important. The conflict curator is present, among others. in criminal proceedings in cases of domestic violence. He represents a child in a situation where parents are conflicted or when one parent dominates another and thus parents cannot properly represent the legal and actual interests of the child. In accordance with the proposed amendments, a guardian representing the child may be appointed a lawyer or legal counsel who has a special knowledge of matters relating to the child of a kind corresponding to the case in which the child is required to be represented or has completed appropriate training on the principles of representation of the child, the rights or the needs of the child. No exceptions are foreseen in criminal matters. It should also be noted that the proposed legislation, which is an absolute novelty in the existing legislation, also provides for the curator's obligation, among others. to get to know the child and to inform him, if, of course, his mental development, his health and degree of maturity allow him to do so, about his actions. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! Changing the appropriate standard of representation of children in court proceedings and proceedings before other authorities is a necessity. Thus, this draft amendment to the Family and Care Code Act should be considered legitimate and the Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The goal of every human being, every child, every mother and father is to establish the biological links existing between the relevant persons that arise as a result of the birth of the child. The system of determining the origin of the child in particular with regard to the father is essentially based on a system of presumption of origin of the child from the husband of the mother. However, it happens in various situations that things can be different. Thus, the law provides for the possibility to challenge the relationship between the relevant persons as a consequence of the birth of the child in marriage. An insight into the right of a man to establish parenthood according to reality and the welfare of the family created by the mother also when the child dies, for the sake of the family and the supposed father, and also as a consequence of this. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u162	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Secondly, the second of the judgments is to include requirements for curators appointed pursuant to Article. 99 k.r.o. Only a lawyer or counsel may be appointed in accordance with the principle. Those who are associated with the structures of the bar or counsel authority should also be entitled to complete appropriate training on the principles of the child's representation, rights or needs, or to have a special knowledge of matters relating to the child in general corresponding to a case in which there is a need to establish a probation officer. In less complex cases, it may also be another person with legal education and a knowledge of the needs of the child, in particular when the custody court sets out in detail the content of the proceedings and, if special circumstances so warrant, another person with a knowledge of the needs of the child. Thirdly, the proposal imposes an obligation on the probation officer to inform the parent who is not in the proceedings and who is taking care of the child, as well as to inform the parent who is not in the proceedings. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u163	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position towards the Government's draft law amending the Family and Care Code Act and the Civil Procedure Code Act following the work on this project in the Extraordinary Commission for Changes in Codifications. The amendments proposed to the draft during the committee's work were not substantive but merely editorial or legislative. As I have already pointed out in my previous position, I believe that these solutions are right and worthy of support. The amended amendment is a consequence of the obligation to enforce the judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal, specifically the judgment of 26 November 2013, and aims inter alia. to make it possible to deny the fatherhood and motherhood after the death of the child when it died after the initiation of the proceedings. With this solution, it will be possible to complete the pending cases if the child has died during their lifetime. In the current legal order, only the prosecutor has such a right, but I see no reason why the alleged parents of a child would be deprived of that right. In addition, the provisions proposed in the amendment provide for the implementation of the provisions of the Constitutional Tribunal of 11 February 2014 concerning the clarification of the powers and formal requirements for curators representing a minor in criminal matters in a conflict of interest between his parents. It is clear from my view that the obligation to provide information to parents who take care of the child is an obligation on these persons, as it consequently strengthens the position of parents in the proper care of the child. The provisions proposed in this amendment are thoughtful, consistent and correctly integrated into the existing system, as confirmed by the opinion of the Legislative Council. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of this project.

This extension will be valid until 30 June 2021. The Director of the National Health Fund will address requests to healthcare providers with whom he will communicate, whether they will continue to wish to provide these benefits. I'm sure you will. This applies to all contracts that have not been terminated on that day. As a large number of contracts expire on 31 December 2019, this would require a great deal of effort from the Fund. At the same time, the Network Act causes hospitals to provide both fixed and outpatient benefits. Therefore, as regards the monitoring by the Minister for Health of this network, it will be time to decide on new regulations by 2021. Until then, it will be possible to extend contracts with providers who provide AOS benefits. Therefore, such a solution is justified. The committee therefore accepted this amendment. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u166	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. As the first Mrs Bernadette Krynick, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u167	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club Law and Justice a position regarding the government draft law amending the Act on Patients' Rights and the Ombudsman of Patient Rights, prints 3176 and 3397. The draft amendment of the law prohibits the charging of hospital or other hospital charges for fixed and 24-hour health benefits from parents or carers staying there with a child or a disabled person to a significant extent. In the version addressed to the Sejm, the project provided that it would be possible to charge for meals given to parents or carers, to use hospital beds, to use an extra bed made available by a hospital or other medical facility. The current version of the draft law drawn up before the subcommittee and adopted by the Health Commission at its meeting on 24 April 2019 no longer includes the abovementioned. exemptions from the principle prohibiting the charging of hospital or other medical facilities providing fixed and 24-hour health benefits from parents or carers staying there with a child or a disabled person to a significant extent. The regulation meets the expectations of parents. It is about the right of the child to have parents or caregivers with him at all times. The presence of loved ones is beneficial for the child. It accelerates the treatment process, relieves stress and strengthens your sense of security. Parents should therefore be able to create as convenient conditions as possible for hospital stays with their children, encourage and facilitate the implementation of this right. However, the collection of parental fees by hospitals limits many of them to the exercise of this right. At present, the fees charged by hospitals are very diverse and, although generally not high, they can become a serious burden on the family budget if they stay longer. The new regulation is intended to facilitate the most common and unlimited contact between hospitalized children and their loved ones. The law and justice stand in the position that there are values and good things that cannot be converted into money. They certainly include hospital care for children and people with disabilities by the people closest to them. Hence, our firm support for the solution that parents and carers cannot be charged for hospital stays and care for their loved ones. This is also a response to the provisions contained in the European Charter of Children's Rights at the Hospital, one of which states that parents' stay should not expose them to additional costs and loss of earnings. Our job is to create conditions that will encourage parents to take care of their children during their stay in the hospital, which will give them minimal relief in their difficult situation. Parents, through their commitment to care for the child, help not only him but also the entire hospital staff, and we can do something to help these parents. In addition, during the meeting of the Health Committee on 24 April this year. a new article has been introduced into the draft law. 2. under which, at the request of the Director of the competent branch of the voivodship National Health Fund, with the agreement of the provider, the application of health care contracts in the field of outpatient specialised care implemented on the date of entry into force of this Act may be extended for a period not longer than 30 June 2021. This proposal will be addressed by the Head of the Regional Branch of the National Health Fund to all healthcare providers who perform health care contracts in the field of outpatient specialized care that have not been terminated at the date of submission of the application. Minister! Dear Marshal, High Izbo! In view of this position, the Law and Justice Club recommends the adoption by the Sejm of a draft law amending the Act on the Rights of the Patient and the Ombudsperson of the Patients, prints 3176 and 3397. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Polish law is inconsistent in this case and clearly makes life difficult for citizens. This project fixes this state of affairs. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u212	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the Government's draft law on the performance of economic activities in the field of the manufacture and marketing of explosives, weapons, ammunition and military or police-purpose products and technology. Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs for first reading on 3 April 2019. The contested Act on Production and Trading is intended to replace the current rules in this area and implement a number of EU regulations into the Polish legal order. One of the main objectives is to introduce into the Polish legal system regulations resulting from the EU directive on control of the acquisition and possession of weapons. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, starting its work on this draft law on 11 April, has decided to set up an emergency subcommittee for its consideration, given the extensive nature of the document. As part of the work on this project, the subcommittee met three times at multi-hour meetings. Multi-hours, because work on the project, which should be stressed, took place with a wide range of social players, i.e. representatives of licensed economic operators involved in the production and marketing of special production and non-governmental organisations. It is also worth noting that social actors had the opportunity to participate in the discussion and comment during the work of the subcommittee. When discussing the text of the Act, it should be noted first of all that it does not introduce diametric changes to the Polish legal order in the field of regulated economic activity related to the trafficking of arms and ammunition and explosives. The main change is the creation of an electronic Weapons Registration System. Such a system will allow to trace the history of each unit of firearms from the moment of their manufacture, through its possession by individual users, until its actual destruction. To serve this is to strengthen the control of the circulation of firearms in Poland. During the work of the subcommittee, Members introduced a number of changes to the bill following a debate between Polish entrepreneurs, the government side and, of course, Members. An important change from the point of view of the users of black powder weapons is the preservation of the existing nomenclature for replicas of such weapons. As a result, it has also been consistent with the provisions of the current Arms and Ammunition Act, and it has been ensured that the availability of such weapons will be regulated on the existing basis. The issue of marking weapons, which has been regulated in Article, has been particularly hotly discussed. 44 of this law. Finally, following the provision in Directive 2017/853 in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 (a) (i) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) and, in its Polish, English and German wording, referring precisely to this article and its literal wording, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs has decided to amend the article. 44 arms labelling laws in such a way that they correspond literally to the provisions of the directive. As a result, Polish producers will not have to mark all relevant parts on each unit of firearms, but will only be obliged to mark each unit of firearms or each substantial part of weapons placed on the market separately. This is an important change for Polish producers, because such a provision will not adversely affect the competitiveness of the production process. At the meeting of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, one of the major changes was also adopted in relation to the original text of the Act, which provided that firearms manufactured for the purposes of the Armed Forces and other uniform formations would not be introduced or processed into the Weapons Registration System. The SRB will therefore only collect information on weapons for civil use and its users. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u214	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Proceded law, so-called. The Act on Production and Trading is intended to replace the current rules in this field and implement a number of EU regulations into the Polish legal order. One of its main objectives is to implement those regulations, which result from the famous EU directive on control of the acquisition and possession of weapons, i.e. Directive 2017/853. When discussing the text of the bill, it should be emphasized first of all that it does not introduce any violent revolution into the Polish legal order concerning regulated economic activity related to the trafficking of arms and ammunition and explosives. Both the government and the Members who worked on this bill followed a principle that could be defined as a directive plus zero. It means introducing only statutory amendments which result directly from EU rules which Poland is required to implement, while avoiding additional regulations. In addition, it should also be pointed out that those who worked on the final version of this document - both Members and the Government - also made efforts to take the interests of the Polish state and Polish entrepreneurs first into account when constructing legislation in this area and at the same time to comply with the letter of law contained in the directive. It should also be stressed here that the bill has undergone a wide public consultation with licensed entrepreneurs and NGOs. The representatives of the social party also participated in the debate held during the meetings of the Emergency Sub-Committee set up for the examination of this draft law and the meeting of the Administrative and Internal Affairs Committee. Therefore, the final version of the project should be considered as the common forces of the government, Members and the social side. When discussing the text of the bill, the shooters should also be calmed down first. Ladies and gentlemen! There is no attempt in this project to smuggle the provisions of the directive into the Arms and Ammunition Act. Black powder lovers can also breathe. The Act does not apply to historical weapons, that is to say, separate charge weapons, designed for firing only using black powder, which was manufactured before 1900. Since this law does not apply to this weapon, it will not need to be marked in accordance with the requirements introduced. Moreover, thanks to the solutions provided for by Members, the naming of black powder weapons remains unchanged and is consistent with what is applied in the arms and ammunition law. The fundamental change introduced by this law is the creation of an electronic Weapons Registration System which will allow to track the history of each unit of firearms from the moment of its manufacture or import by individual users until it is destroyed. It should also be noted that the firearms which will be manufactured for the purposes of the Armed Forces and other uniform formations will not be introduced or processed into the Weapons Registration System. This will collect information only on weapons for civil use and its users. The exclusion of these weapons from the SRB, the one which is intended for the Armed Forces and other uniform formations, should be considered to be beneficial for the interests of our country. And that is why I am very pleased that this change has been able to finally push through and agree with the government, to introduce at the last meeting of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u222	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The operation of the Act of 22 June 2001 on the pursuit of economic activities in the field of the manufacture and marketing of explosives, weapons, ammunition and military or police-purpose products and technology has contributed well to the regulation of this very important area over the 18 years of its existence. However, in the meantime there were external conditions which made it necessary to adapt Polish legal standards to the regulation of acts of international law, such as the regulation implementing the Article. 10 of the United Nations Protocol supplementing the United Nations Convention, as well as Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2015/2003 of 15 December 2015 laying down common guidelines on standards and techniques for the removal of weapons of performance in order to ensure that non-commercial weapons are permanently unfit for use. This new comprehensive legal act includes provisions laying down rules on the taking up and pursuit of economic activities by economic operators to the same extent as in the title of the Act and, importantly, lays down rules on the purification of sites from explosives and hazardous materials. The draft law, following its work and in-depth analysis during the work of the Administrative and Internal Affairs Commission, clearly and clearly identifies the issue of the manufacture of explosives, weapons, ammunition and military or police-purpose products and technologies and their marketing. It is worth noting that standards are laid down in the field of mediation, commercial advice, as well as assistance in the conclusion of contracts and the organisation of shipments of explosives, weapons and ammunition. The project clearly defines terms such as ammunition, weapons, firearms, essential parts, essential parts of artillery firearms, barrels or flames. The legislator in the project also specifies several exceptions when the law does not apply. This refers to historical weapons, firearms included in the inventory of museums, as well as weapons not capable of projecting missiles using the energy of products resulting from the chemical reaction of the propelling charge and the cross-section of weapons to present the way the weapons mechanisms work. Particular attention is paid to the fact that the legislator lays down rules on the licensing of economic activities in the area covered by the project, which seems to be particularly important, because special importance is attached to this issue, which means that the licensing authority will be notified in an absolute manner subject to authorisation by the relevant authorities of the State. In order to implement this provision, the conditions, procedures and procedures for the concession awarded shall be clearly defined, paying particular attention to the importance and importance of the medical certificate and psychological decision. Attention should be paid to the fact that the wording of the Article is made. 22 dealing with the refusal of the concession award by the licensing authority, which is of great importance. The regulated regulations define precisely and rigorously the obligations imposed on the trader, as is treated by Section III. At this point, the definition of the process of trafficking in explosives, weapons and ammunition should be raised, giving special powers to customs and treasury authorities. The export and transit of firearms, parts and essential components and ammunition to firearms will be subject to special regulation, which is strictly governed by international and Union law. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u246	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! It is my pleasure to present the position of the Modern Club regarding this bill. It is a good thing that in this case, almost four years later, there is a situation like this. As regards the reservations of the Constitutional Tribunal, it was basically one thing, namely that if members of cooperatives who do not participate and are not interested in taking part in the conversion of cooperatives into a company governed by commercial law, they may receive part of the resources available to the cooperative but up to 10%. These measures. The Court considered this unfair because these measures should be at least in the amount that the person concerned has invested as a contribution to the cooperative. The situation is that a person who agrees to transfer the value of these shares to a newly created commercial law company gets the funds in full. The resources at its disposal shall be rewritten as a share in the company under commercial law. And the person who is not interested in it gets a much smaller amount, because only 10% These measures. It was therefore appropriate to refer this objection to the Constitutional Court, which considered that these provisions were indeed incorrect. Therefore, the cooperative law removes the section on this particular issue. Therefore, there is nothing left to do but actually adopt this law so that it fulfils its assumptions regarding the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. As I said at the outset, it was a good thing that the amendment led to the situation that those proceedings which had already been initiated and not completed with an entry in the register of entrepreneurs could have been carried out on the basis of those provisions which had been in force so far. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u253	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Senate draft law amending the Cooperative Law concerns the alignment of the right to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 16 June 2015, K 25/12, and the provisions of the Constitutional Tribunal of 15 July 2015, S 5/15, by excluding the possibility of transforming cooperatives into commercial companies. This means that a change in the way the cooperative operates will only be possible through the prior liquidation of the cooperative. The proposed regulation will increase the regulatory burden since, compared to the current legislation, the transformation of labour cooperatives into a commercial company will require additional tasks set out in cooperative law, and consequently will extend the process of transforming labour cooperatives into an entity governed by commercial law. This regulation will apply to a relatively small sector and will have a positive impact as the rights of cooperative members will be better protected. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development recommends the adoption of a law amending the law on cooperative law. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club supports the proposed changes.

Ladies and gentlemen, first of all, we are introducing far-reaching changes to the general section of the Criminal Code, which regulates the general principles of the penalty. Today, the penalty in the Penal Code is quite rigidly imposed: courts can rule sentences between 1 month and 15 years of imprisonment, 25 years of imprisonment and life imprisonment. In fact, life imprisonment is a life sentence by name alone. In fact, in today's circumstances, there is no way to prosecute a life sentence. We want to change this and introduce such a penalty with this amendment. The most important change we are introducing is the possibility for the courts to rule in a more flexible way, namely from 1 month to 25 years of imprisonment. We're eliminating 25 years of imprisonment. What does that mean? This means that today the courts, ruling penalties e.g. for cruel rape, they cannot punish 17 or 27 years of imprisonment because they are bound by such rigid restrictions. Thanks to our changes, they will be able to do so and properly and adequately assess the guilt and evil committed by the perpetrator of the crime. It can be different, especially if the perpetrators committed acts in multiplayer configurations, they acted in a criminal group. Their role may have been different and then the very severe punishment imposed should be adapted to the guilt and evil for which individual perpetrators are responsible. In addition to this fundamental change in the general section of the Penal Code, which also introduces, as I mentioned earlier, the possibility of life imprisonment, we introduce in the Code a number of amendments which order the courts to take greater account of the aggravating circumstances that often escape them in the execution of punishment, especially when we are dealing with these cruel, brutal crimes. We are making far-reaching changes when it comes to punishing the perpetrators of the redecisive, namely those who return to the way of crime, and especially those who do so once again. [[To teenagers.]] Today they are treated very gently. It can be said that the severity of the penalties for them is purely symbolic. That's gonna change radically. The changes we are making are also intended to significantly limit the possibility of conditional early release of perpetrators, especially those who have no hope of improvement and who can be expected to return to crime. The courts will be armed with additional tools they don't have today. This will serve security, among others. children. Regardless of the changes in the general section of the Criminal Code, we also make a number of changes to the special section, the one that regulates specific records striking criminals. Today, the penalty provided for in the Penal Code for rape is between three and 15 years of imprisonment. If this is rape with special cruelty, the penalty is between 5 and 15 years imprisonment. Change is fundamental: we introduce a number of types of actions, one can say that some kind of casuistics. ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u393	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law on which we are proceeding is intended to implement into the legal order the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 28 June 2016. The Constitutional Tribunal found the provisions of the Enforcement Act in the administration incompatible with the Constitution, which do not specify the upper limit of the handling fee and one of the fees for enforcement: the seizure of the monetary claim. In addition, they require that enforcement fees be levied in full also in the event of voluntary payment by the obliged party. In its judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal recognised the need to analyse the whole system of enforcement costs in order to improve those provisions. At the same time, the judgment of the Constitutional Court did not repeal the provisions currently in force but ordered them to be amended. At the moment, the situation is that both the handling fee and all enforcement fees are charged on a percentage basis. The amount shall be subject to the amount of the claim to be recovered. For example, the handling fee is 1% the amount of that claim, i.e. the nominal amount of the claim to be recovered. Enforcement fees range from 4% to 10% the amount to be recovered, depending on the enforcement action taken by the administrative executioner. It is important that the fee applies to each enforcement operation, regardless of whether the execution is effective. Regardless of whether the amounts to be enforced are collected, the fee shall be charged and the fee shall in principle be charged to the obliged person, but in many cases, where the sums cannot be recovered from the obliged person, it shall be charged to the creditor. The proposed law creates a new system of enforcement costs, in which the amount of the handling fee depends on the expenditure incurred by the authorities until the start of the execution, i.e. a fee which essentially concerns the material and technical activities of the enforcement authority which are related to the costs incurred in connection with the enforcement and enforcement service. Secondly, the amount of enforcement fees will be linked to the effect of enforcement, i.e. will depend on the amount of the duty actually incurred. In the submitted project, we provide for two quota rates of handling fee. The enforcement authority will charge 40 PLN for the preliminary examination of the implementing title when initiating proceedings. It will be a fee corresponding to the cost of verification of documents, the data of the obliged person, the assessment of the formal correctness of the implementing title, the completeness of the applications. When enforcement proceedings are initiated, this fee will increase to PLN 100 and this amount will be charged as a fixed amount, covering administrative costs related to operations such as the collection of information on the assets of the obliged person, i.e. the request for assets, and sometimes devoted to the verification of assets of the obliged person in the information systems available to the enforcement authority. We propose that the amount of the enforcement fee should be uniform and be 10% the funds enforced and its upper limit was 40 000. zł. We also propose that, in the case of voluntary payment of the amounts sought by the obliged person, it be possible to reduce this fee - to encourage the obliged person to settle the debt as soon as possible - to 5% the amount recovered, with a limit of not more than 20 000. PLN in this case. We propose to extend the catalog of enforcement expenses, such as the cost of commuting the collector to the obliged person, correspondence costs, transport costs of occupied movable property. We would like to add to these costs a court fee for the entry in the perpetual ledger or the entry in the National Court Register for the initiation of execution, in particular where this applies to the entry in the book of perpetual compulsory mortgage that protects the debts of Member States and third countries. Poland, as a member of the European Community and as a country which is dating other countries on legal aid, is required in some cases also to execute foreign claims. We want the execution costs to be preventive. It's about knowing that he's gonna pay the execution costs. The change we are introducing here will consist in the fact that the amount of enforcement costs will already be indicated in a reminder, so he will be able to respond even before enforcement proceedings are initiated and for example. obtain this reduction, which I mentioned, i.e. will then pay half of the execution costs, not the full amount of enforcement costs, i.e. 5% instead of 10%. The project envisages a change in the cost relief system, provides for remission, distribution into instalments. This is theoretically possible at the moment, but the rules are somewhat inconsistent in this case, because what the enforcement authority can do is it does on the basis of, say, the proper application of the Public Finance Act. We want this issue to be regulated in the Enforcement Act in the administration and that the rules directly constitute the conditions for reducing these costs, for cutting these costs, for granting reliefs, and for the Enforcement Act in the administration to have its own state aid rules. We also propose the introduction of rules governing the limitation of the costs of reminders and enforcement costs. Currently, there is no separate regulation on the limitation of enforcement costs and the costs of notice, the tax administration applies on a daily basis, which makes the same provisions applicable to those claims as to the obligations which are subject to investigation. Our proposal is that we will introduce a uniform 3-year limitation period for the costs of reminders and enforcement costs. The project also provides for the waiver of the bail-in fee. This is the fee that is collected at the end of enforcement operations when the creditor receives from the enforcement authority the amount of the funds enforced. The amount of the bail-in fee shall be 5%. the amounts transferred to the creditor. We propose that this fee be removed from the enforcement system. We are also introducing some simplifications regarding the reopening of the administrative execution procedure. Currently, a new enforcement title is the basis for reopening the administrative execution. These are certain formalities that the creditor must carry out. We propose that enforcement proceedings may be reopened if it is established that the assets of the obliged person are somewhere, only on the basis of a repeated notification to the obliged person that this procedure will continue. We are also introducing a novelty to facilitate the fulfilment of those obligations that are being incurred in the execution process. We propose that we introduce a circle of entities that can pay a monetary amount for the obliged person. This will also apply to interest, of course also to the cost of warning and enforcement costs. The payment for the obliged person will be able to make the debtor in kind, e.g. the current owner of the property charged with a compulsory mortgage to which the execution is directed, or the owner of the movable property charged with the tax pledge, i.e. a similar situation where the person is interested in ending the execution, especially if he wants to sell the mortgaged property. We propose - and this is an important novel - the introduction of the possibility of payment by the people closest to the obliged person, i.e. by a spouse, children, parents or siblings. This, of course, is voluntary. We will only honor such payment if it is clearly stated that the person closest to us regulates the obligation for someone else, for a family member, so that it is fully clear that this person regulates someone else's obligations. In other cases, it will also be possible, but only up to 5,000. zł, i.e. it will be possible to pay for the obliged person, but only up to the amount of 5 thousand. zł. In the transitional provisions, we provide in enforcement proceedings initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Act for the collection of existing enforcement costs, but with a reduction in their amount, i.e. here with the introduction of this limitation, which the Court questioned. The handling fee will be charged in no amount higher than PLN 100 and a residual fee of not more than PLN 40 000. zł. We propose that the amended provisions enter into force 18 months after the date of the announcement, and in the particular case of the bail-in fee that we are removing, it will cease to apply from 1 January of the year following the entry into force of the Act. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u394	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Waldemar Bud will speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u395	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present an opinion, a position on the Law amending the Law on Enforcement in Administration and some other laws. Ladies and gentlemen! The amendment of the law follows the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 28 June 2016, which pointed out the need to amend the rules, generally speaking, precisely on enforcement fees, while the amendment itself goes much further, it is systemic in its approach to changes in the charging of enforcement fees in the administration, completely redesigning these issues. First of all, the Court questioned the situation in which the enforcement fee does not have a maximum value and does not depend on the action, which is not linked to the costs of enforcement proceedings in the administration. Of course, the project responds to this, essentially remodeling it. Firstly, a levy shall be cancelled which does not take into account the possible collection of the claim. This 10% charge depends on efficiency, i.e. the collection of part or all of the charges. Of course, there's an upper limit. We can't even talk about tens of thousands of fees here in the absence of action or efficiency. The maximum fee is 40,000. PLN and, of course, meets the expectations of the tribunal. A number of changes also apply to the handling fee, which is relatively low today, in quota terms. All these changes are remodeling the system. They move towards efficiency and execute the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal. For these reasons, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support them. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u396	Thank you. Ms Katarzyna Osos, Civic Platform Club - Civic Coalition. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u397	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Club to present a position towards the government draft law amending the Law on Enforcement in Administration and some other laws, print no.3379. By judgment of 28 June 2016, the Constitutional Tribunal ruled that the provisions on enforcement in the administration were incompatible with the Constitution, in so far as they did not specify the maximum amount of the enforcement fee and the handling fee respectively, and in so far as they did not provide for the possibility of reducing the enforcement fee and the handling fee in the event of the withdrawal of the proceedings due to the voluntary payment of the enforcement fee after enforcement. At the same time, the Court pointed out that, for the proper implementation of the judgment, the legislator should determine not only the maximum amount of those fees but also the maximum amount of other enforcement fees, and also how these fees should be calculated in the event of redemption of the proceedings in connection with the voluntary payment of the enforcement fee after enforcement. Thus, the proposed regulation provides for the setting of two amounts of the handling fee, regardless of the amount of the debt covered by the implementing title to be collected separately from each implementing title, the introduction of a enforcement fee levied in proportion to the cash being enforced, the reduction in the amount of the enforcement fee in case of voluntary payment of the enforcement fee, the non-imposition of enforcement costs of a creditor operating within a State budgetary unit or a State budgetary unit, the extension of the catalogue of enforcement expenditure, the amendment of the conditions for the remission of enforcement costs and the payment of those costs in instalments, the introduction of provisions governing the limitation of enforcement costs and the cost of enforcement, the withdrawal of the bail-in fee, and the revision of procedures for the re-launch of administrative executions resulting from the amended provisions on enforcement costs. High Izbo! As a club, we are in favour of putting the project into further work. The judgments of the tribunal must, of course, be enforced. A number of comments were submitted to the project during the consultation. Some of them have been taken into account. During the committee's work, it will be possible to re-examine the comments not included so that - at least the Confederation of Leviathan - the fees are not separated from the actual expenditure incurred by enforcement authorities and do not form part of the additional penalty. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u398	Thank you. I invite Mr Paweł Grabowski, the Kukiz Club15. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u399	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 against the government draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings in the administration and some other laws, print no. 3379. Ladies and gentlemen! This law is intended to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Court. The Court found that the absence of a maximum enforcement fee and the absence of an indication of the method of calculating handling fees were incompatible with the Constitution. So here in this case, the project is not controversial. For the sake of clarity, the Kukiz Club will offer its Members support and further work on the project in committee. However, it is controversial that such a law has been in operation for years in the Polish legal system. It is interesting that we are dealing with hundreds of different legal changes of less and more importance, and it turns out that there are no clear rules in enforcement proceedings in the administration, according to which fees such as handling fees are set or execution fees. These are, after all, charges that citizens have to pay out of their own pockets, or more graphically, with their hard-earned money. The question can therefore be asked: Is a country in which there are no specific rules, and the rules that exist are implemented on the basis of one I see, a state of law? So, ladies and gentlemen, in order not to prolong, we are in favour of sending the project to the committee. The Poselski Kukiz Club15 will recommend to its Members that they opt for further proceding on this project. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u400	I invite Mrs Genowefa Tokarska to speak on behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u401	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present my position towards the government's draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings in the administration and some other laws, print no.3379. As my previous speakers said, the draft law is, of course, a response to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of June 2016 concerning enforcement proceedings in the administration. The Constitutional Tribunal pointed out the need for the legislator to intervene in determining the amount of the enforcement and handling fee and to determine how those fees should be calculated also in the event of the cancellation of the enforcement procedure. As has been said, the Minister spoke of it. The project provides for the setting of two rates of handling fee. The first, the lower, of PLN 40 on each implementing title, regardless of the amount covered by the title, was charged for the fact that enforcement proceedings were initiated. The second, in the amount of PLN 100, is collected as soon as the execution begins or starts. The project also provides for a 10% enforcement fee the amount of cash executed, but not more than 40 000. zł, but already in the case of voluntary payment of charges there is a reduction in the amount of the enforcement fee to 5% the funds obtained and up to a maximum of 20 000. zł. The project rightly exempts from enforcement costs a creditor operating within a national budgetary unit or a state budgetary unit. In addition, a catalogue of enforcement expenditure, such as e.g. the costs of correspondence, the costs of court proceedings relating to the entry for the seizure of claims in the permanent register or the entry in the National Court Register. The list of enforcement expenses also includes reasonable costs related to e.g. with the assistance of the enforcement authority by the services: Police, Border Guard, ABW or Intelligence Agency. The obligation to pay enforcement expenditure shall not arise when the monetary amount, interest and costs of the notice are paid or remitted in full after the execution procedure has been initiated, but definitely before the administrative execution itself has been initiated. It should be noted that, in principle, at each stage of the enforcement procedure, the project gives the debtor such an opportunity to reduce enforcement costs in the event of voluntary payment of the debt. This applies also to handling fees, and enforcement fees, and other enforcement costs. It must be acknowledged that these regulations provide incentives for voluntary payment of charges. The bill also abolishes the bail-in fee applied in principle Only by the chiefs of the IRS. Moreover, of course, it will have an impact on the public finance sector. The annual revenue of the state budget will increase by PLN 333 million. The annual revenue of local government units acting as enforcement authorities, because not all of them have such functions, will increase by 10 million. The annual revenue of the ZUS will increase by PLN 75.4 million. In this respect, it should be noted that these revenues are no longer valid for the coming year after the introduction of the law and will be achieved in the third year of the law. Five regulations of the Minister of Finance are also an integral part of the project, including a proposal for printing. In conclusion, the draft law under discussion takes into account the observations made by the Constitutional Court's judgment, introducing very detailed rules on enforcement proceedings in the administration. We express our opinion on the project's referral to further work in Parliament. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u411	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! As regards your questions, I would like to reassure you that we treat the handling fee as an administrative fee, as a bank charge for checking a credit claim. We've calculated how much it costs us to do these preliminary tasks, e.g. the determination of the obliged person. We've calculated how much it costs. On average, 68 zlotys come out. And that's the amount that we kind of weighed. We have this 40 PLN at the time of the examination of this implementing title. If the obliged person pays by then, it is 40 PLN. If there are further steps, i.e. you will have to submit a motion for enforcement, this handling fee will increase to PLN 100, so it will not be 40 plus 100, only 40 plus 60, which will be PLN 100 in total. And in a medium way, it reflects the costs that we bear in these handling activities at the beginning. The question was whether the handling fee would raise the awareness of debtors. As far as consciousness is concerned, in general, when we start execution proceedings and someone doesn't know they're obliged, they get reprimanded. So this model that you have to send a reminder to the obliged person exists. The creditor must do so in order to make a request to the enforcement authority at all. Documents are coming to the enforcement authority. One of them must be a confirmation that he has received a warning that he has been reprimanded to pay. I don't think it's easy to talk about a situation like this that someone won't know that he's in danger of being executed. Mrs Czernow asked whether enforcement fees are not too high, whether such excessive fiscalism is possible as regards the possibility of these charges. That's how I'd explain it. The current situation is that these fees are levied relatively, i.e. in amounts of 4, 5, 6 or 10% from any execution operation. It doesn't matter if this enforcement action, at all, the entire execution will lead to the collection of any amounts. It is therefore a question of putting in place a guarantee that the fee will depend on the actual effects. If the obliged person pays something, a fee of 10% will be charged. But from the amount we've downloaded. In the current system, the fee is charged on the amount of the denomination of the undertaking. This means that it is downloaded regardless of the effects. It is necessary to adopt some criteria for this investigation of these enforcement fees. We also need to remember that it's not like someone didn't voluntarily pay something, didn't know. This is a proceeding that aims to enforce the law. If someone doesn't pay, you pay, you pay, we all pay. It means that we bear the costs, all those who normally pay taxes or some of their debts, their liabilities. In fact, we keep this camera to get money from this unreliable debtor. And these execution costs can in many cases be covered. There could be a situation where we don't download anything and we don't cover those costs. It's calculated to cover those execution costs. Unfortunately, that's what it costs. I do not see any justification for the State to bear the costs of what is necessary in order to comply with the obligation by execution, i.e. by compulsory removal. And the last question about voluntary repayment. I mentioned this during the presentation of the bill. Voluntary payment involves a reduction in costs. [[But the bailiffs, not the administration.]] In the administration. I can answer for that. On the other hand, when it comes to bailiffs, the question is rather to the justice department, which, I understand, deals with bailiffs, because they are supervised by the Minister of Justice. We're in charge of administrative execution. We have proposed a solution which, in our view, is an appropriate solution. The point is that voluntary repayment reduces those costs which are currently not subject to voluntary repayment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u413	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Poselski Club I am pleased to present the position of the club regarding the government draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings in the administration and some other laws, print no. 3379. The aim of the proposed proposal is to bring the law into line with the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 28 June 2016 on non-compliance with Article. Article 2 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. 64 § 1 point 4 and § 6 the Law of 17 June 1966 on enforcement proceedings in the administration. This project aims to determine not only the maximum amount of the enforcement fee referred to in Article. 64 § 1 point 4 and the handling fee referred to in Article. 64 § 6, but also the maximum amount of other enforcement fees. In addition, it is intended to determine how these fees are to be calculated in the event of redemption of the proceedings in connection with the voluntary payment of the enforcement fee after enforcement. The objectives set are to be met by setting two amounts of the handling fee, regardless of the amount of the duty covered by the implementing title, to be collected separately from each implementing title: 40 PLN for the initiation of enforcement proceedings, which is increased to 100 PLN when administrative execution is initiated, and by introducing instead enforcement fees levied in proportion to the amount of 10% of the funds enforced amounts of cash executed but not more than PLN 40,000. In addition, the project submitted reduces the enforcement fee by half in case of voluntary payment of the enforcement fee. Unfortunately, I have reservations about the inappropriate way of regulating the limitation period for the costs of warning and its detachment from the calculation of the costs of warning and too complicated and difficult to determine when the limitation period begins. The amount of the reminder costs should not exceed the existence of the principal. We hope that this will be changed in the course of the Committee's further work on the draft submitted. In the course of the public consultation, it was requested to introduce the possibility for the tax collector to collect the amounts recovered through a mobile terminal using a payment card, provided that this is the implementation of the enforcement measure. Although this proposal does not constitute the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Court, the execution of which is the subject of the proposal submitted, it must be taken into account. The Ministry shall ensure that a separate draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings in the administration is prepared for this proposal. For the stability of the Polish legal system, it is better for one law to be amended by one comprehensive law, not a series of successive projects. Unfortunately, we are used to the fact that even two projects amending the same law may be carried out during one session of the Sejm. I hope that the next proposal for amendments to the Enforcement Act in the administration will be much broader and will also take into account the proposals for changes made during the public consultation to the current proposal. Members of the Committee will vote in favour of the proposal submitted for further work in committee.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-05-15-sejm-81-1.u437	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice for print no. 3450. This is a report by the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Employee Capital Plans. Ladies and gentlemen! I'll start with a shortcut like this. What is PPK? In short, it may even be a second pension for Poles. The employer, the state and the employee themselves consist of savings collected in a special account, which the Pole will be able to use after 60 years of age. In this way, today's 20-year-olds, 30-year-olds, 40-year-olds, and even 50-year-olds after their career, will have additional money, from several hundred zlotys to several thousand zlotys a month, depending on how many years they saved and how much they earned. It is a solidarity social programme which strengthens the financial security of Polish families. Each Pole, I think, realizes that we are on the eve of a serious debate about the pension system. It was yesterday at the committee meeting that Minister Emilewicz also said. The demographic changes facing Poland, which are inevitable, will result in a dramatic change in the ratio of the number of workers who pay contributions to the number of pensioners in 20 and 30 years. So these employee capital plans will be a security for future pensioners, they will really be the safety of their old age. Ladies and gentlemen! This law was adopted as early as October and is now being amended so that it can be implemented even more efficiently. The government has noticed some problems. One of the problems, the main problem that this law resolves, is the matter of 30 times the average salary, such a limitation which is in the PPK and which is de facto unnecessary and only hinders the implementation of this law. So this will be eliminated, and this 30 times the base of the PPK will be abolished. There's also a few minor changes. The catalogue of persons employed within the meaning of the PPK Act is extended to persons who are on parental leave or who are receiving maternity benefits. It is possible to use a mechanical signature to call on the employer to conclude a PPK management agreement. Additional opportunities to inform PPK participants are introduced by providing them with access to the IT system of the financial institution. PPK accounts are excluded from the obligation to identify and report within the meaning of the Tax Information Exchange Act with other countries. In order to ensure the efficiency of the investments made, an additional condition is introduced for the remuneration of the PPK managers for the result achieved at a level of not less than 75% the highest rate of return under the funds of the same defined date. By changing the definition of a representative trade union organisation, the legal gap is removed and allows the operator of the PPK portal to spend funds from the initial fee and from the annual fee to the representative trade union organisations, at their request, for education and promotion of the PPK. Ladies and gentlemen! In the committee, as the rapporteur said, we accepted a number of amendments tabled by Members of the Law and Justice, but there were also important voices of entrepreneurs, business organisations in this committee. The Polish Bank Association has raised important reservations, important amendments, although they were not put to the vote in committee, the Federation of Polish Entrepreneurs, the Confederation of Leviathan. We leaned over these proposals and actually go out to the entrepreneurs. And they did their job very well, they read the bill, they read it, and they pointed out what else could be improved. And we, in front of entrepreneurs, business organizations, have prepared a few amendments to the bill that I am putting on your marshal's hands right now. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
